# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented here.
Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), with project-specific versioning (`w` = website, `g` = Google Apps Script, `r` = repository). Older sections are rotated to [CHANGELOG-archive.md](CHANGELOG-archive.md) when this file exceeds 100 version sections.

`Sections: 95/100`

### Added
- **`grid-stability-and-the-generator`** (gate `tracks`) — *Inertia, Reactive Power, and Teaching Electronics to Lead*, the **forty-fourth lesson** and **the one that closes the floor track**, authored as `clLessonGridStabilityAndTheGenerator_()` **inside** the `// CONTENT START` … `// CONTENT END` fence, registered at the **end** of `clLessons_()` and **appended** to `electrical-foundations`'s `lessons[]` at §4 position 5 (position 2, `string-versus-central`, is row 15 and unbuilt). Group `Technology Foundations`; `updated` 2026-09-16, `reviewBy` **2027-03-16**; four tiles, a **nine-entry** glossary. Inputs: `study:ge-vernova@2026-08-21` · `study:black-veatch@2026-08-21` · `study:hitachi-energy@2026-08-21` · `study:sinexcel@2026-08-08` · `study:siemens-energy@2026-09-04` · `study:piller@2026-09-03` · `study:power-electronics@2026-09-06` · `concepts:profiler-concepts@2026-09-13`. **All eight public, so the stamp folds to `tracks`** — verified through the real serving path rather than assumed: an analyst session answered `cop=lesson` with all eight sections and saw the lesson in its index with `electrical-foundations`'s `withheld` count at 0
- **A section §3.2's outline does not contain — `two-knobs` (table), inserted between the two `prose` sections.** See `### Changed` for the resolution and its ground. Eight sections in all: `the-spinning-machine` (prose) · `two-knobs` (table, 4 × 3) · `frequency-is-a-meter` (prose) · `what-retires-with-coal` (callout) · `following-versus-forming` (proscons, 2 cards) · `where-it-fails` (callout) · `drill` (8 cards) · `check-yourself` (5 items) — **six distinct kinds and no two `prose` adjacent**

### Changed
- **`repository-information/CLASSROOM-CURRICULUM-PLAN.md` §3.2's seven-id outline became eight, deliberately and with the reason written down.** §3.2 opens `the-spinning-machine` (prose) · `frequency-is-a-meter` (prose) — **two `prose` sections back to back, which §8 item 6 forbids in as many words**. This is a contradiction between two parts of the plan rather than a licence to pick one, and §7.34 wrote out three routes, preferring (b): convert the second to `bars` or `timeline` **only if a source states the numbers**. **Route (b) failed on its own condition.** No input carries a frequency-deviation series, and `study:power-electronics` records in terms that the quantification — *no inertia constant, no fault-current contribution, no RoCoF withstand* — is *in no datasheet, no release and no independent test result*; a `bars` could therefore only have been invented, which §8 item 6 bars explicitly. **Route (a) was taken**: a `table` was inserted between them and the count moved seven → eight. The insert is load-bearing rather than a spacer — `study:ge-vernova` states *one machine, two independent knobs* outright, and `two-knobs`'s third row is the lesson's own hinge (**what you turn: nothing** — the mass is not a control), which is exactly the third, separate loss `what-retires-with-coal` has to be able to name two sections later. **Section ids are permanent, so eight is now the contract**
- **`googleAppsScripts/Classroom/Classroom.gs`** — `clTrackElectricalFoundations_()`'s `lessons[]` appended and its `updated` advanced 2026-09-15 → 2026-09-16, scoped to that literal alone (never a blanket replace — session 9's `guidanceDocEo14420_()` corruption is the precedent); `VERSION` `v01.48g` → `v01.49g`
- **`segment-grid-equipment` and `segment-bridge-and-on-site-generation` regenerated** with `--segment … --today 2026-09-16` and nothing else, the generator reporting `changed: read-next; no pin moved` on both. **The first Phase 4 row to regenerate two segments**
- **`live-site-pages/gs-versions/Classroomgs.version.txt`** → `|v01.49g|`; **`live-site-pages/gs-changelogs/Classroomgs.changelog.md`** one generic section (`New lesson added to a track` / `Curriculum updated`), 48 → 49
- **`INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md`** — §6's Phase 4 row and §7.3's order-6 row flipped to **14 of 26** and dated, with the interleave moved to **14:9**; **§7.35 written in the same commit**, both halves
- **`CLASSROOM-CURRICULUM-PLAN.md`** — §4's track table marks position 5 built, §7 row 14 carries its Built note
- **`README.md`** — `Last updated:`, `Repo version:` to v06.05r, and the Classroom GAS display synced by `check-readme-tree.py --fix`

### Notes
- **BOTH HALVES OF THE DUE COUNT WERE CALLED CORRECTLY, AND THE BRIEF'S DERIVATION OF THE SECTION HALF WAS WRONG.** `--check` ran **10 → 12 → 10** exactly as §7.34 forecast, `read-next` alone on both segments and no deepening. But §7.34 asserted that `grid-stability-and-the-generator` is **not** in `CRITERION_LEXICON`, "checked entry by entry at v06.03r" — **it is**, keyed on `grid-forming`, `inertia`, `reactive`, `stability`, `black start`. `what-is-bought-and-on-what` held still for a different reason: **none of those five keywords appears in either segment's six `buyingCriteria` strings**, checked programmatically rather than by eye. **So row 12's gate needs one more turn**: the section half is bounded by `READ_NEXT` ∩ `CRITERION_LEXICON` **and then by an actual case-insensitive keyword hit against that segment's own `buyingCriteria`** — three tests, not two, and only the third can be run without guessing. A lexicon-membership claim in a brief is not evidence; grep the file
- **THE FORESEEABLE P7 DID NOT FIRE, BECAUSE THE EST DAY HAD ROLLED.** Both segments were last regenerated on 2026-09-15 EST and §7.34 flagged the risk. Read before the run rather than after: both literals carried `"updated": "2026-09-15"`, EST read **2026-09-16**, and both advanced. **`check-classroom-pipeline.py --base origin/main` reported 0 findings on the code-only diff** — not even P1, because at that point the only two changed paths (`Classroom.gs`, `Classroomgs.version.txt`) are both inside the pipeline's own write set; P1 appears only once the plan documents join the diff. The signature is **P1 alone** — no P5 on an append, no P3 (a lesson inside the fence moves no `GATE_SYMBOLS` member, so `gateDigest` was deliberately left untouched), no P7, no P8 (it cannot fire on a head-only lesson)
- **§10.6 (t) PAID A FIFTH TIME, AND THIS ROW PRE-DECLARED THE HANDOFF IN BOTH A TILE AND A NOTE.** Two landscape modules take this lesson as a `READ_NEXT` entry and both name machines and operators it must not. A tile reads **0 vendors named** and the `what-retires-with-coal` note declines the ranking in terms. **The claim was verified rather than asserted**: all seven study guides were scanned for any sizing or ranking of the grid-stability machine and **none carries one**, so "no input to this lesson sizes or ranks that market" is a measurement rather than a posture. Earlier pre-declarations were one tile (session 6) or one callout (session 8); this is the first to use both in one lesson
- **`reviewBy` 2027-03-16 on the ~6-month default, with four rejections in writing.** The fetched corpus holds exactly three future-dated items and none is this lesson's gate. *January 2027* — one vendor's medium-voltage overload ladder becoming available — rejected twice over: a vendor fact in a lesson written vendor-blind, and a product-availability date rather than a rule taking effect. *24 December 2026* — EO 14420's DOE covered-countries deadline — rejected as a manufacturing-origin gate bearing on procurement rather than on stability, and as already `eo14420`'s own clock under the (r)/(u) bar. The **ride-through rulebook** (IEEE 1547-2018, IEEE 2800-2022, FERC Order 901, NERC PRC-029-1, PRC-030-1) is described throughout as adopted and enforceable with **no phase-in or effective date stated anywhere in the fetched corpus** — and reaching outside it for one is what §5.2 forbids. And the segment policy fence is a landscape session's to sort, not a Phase 4 row's. The date keeps §5's due-for-review count at **2**, and it lands beside row 13's 2027-03-15 and `the-transformer-and-the-substation`'s 2027-03-14 — the floor track's three most recent lessons now share a review window, which is coherent rather than accidental
- **THE MICRO-MARKUP CHECK EARNED ITSELF A FOURTH TIME, ON ITS OTHER HALF.** The first draft carried five errors and every one was the `textContent` trap `CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` documents: a `proscons` card's `t` and `meta`, and a `glossary[].d`, are printed as text rather than sent to `clFmt`, so a `{{term}}` in any of them reaches the reader as literal braces. Nothing else in the toolchain sees it, and the rendered page confirmed the fix — **zero surviving `{{…}}` and zero literal asterisks across 26,208 characters**
- **RENDER — all eight sections at analyst, zero page errors, and the negative half is INVERTED on a public lesson.** A `tracks`-gated lesson **is** visible to an analyst, so the test is that it renders rather than that it is refused, and it did: `#cl-<sectionId>` present for all eight, every screenshot read. The two console errors are the standing environmental `ERR_CERT_AUTHORITY_INVALID` pair. **One harness finding worth carrying: `Classroom.html`'s CSP `connect-src` is `'self'` plus Google hosts, so a shim server on another port is REFUSED outright** — the fix is to point `_gasPost` at a same-origin path (`/__gas`) and fulfil it with Playwright's `page.route()`. The `two-knobs` table rendered clean at 1400px, a **seventeenth** data point against the open `.cl-tbl` `min-width` question
- **THE ROTATION FIRED, AND FOR ONCE THE EST/UTC TRAP RESOLVED THE OTHER WAY.** EST read **2026-09-16 00:07** against UTC's **04:07**, so the **twenty-six** sections dated 2026-09-15 all stopped being exempt overnight and the file stood at **106 raw / 106 non-exempt against a 100 trigger** — §7.33's escape clause could not fire. The **2026-09-07 group (13 sections, `v05.07r` … `v05.20r`)** moved to `CHANGELOG-archive.md`, **all thirteen SHA-resolved** on a 1,269-commit clone, and the mandatory post-rotation grep passed on the first attempt. The file closes at **94 raw**, one of which (this section) is today's — so it reads 94 non-exempt tomorrow and the next group in line, **2026-09-08 (10 sections)**, does not arm until the file is back at 100. `Classroomgs.changelog.md` closes at **49/50** and does **not** rotate: its own new section is same-day exempt. `Scrapergs.changelog.md` 31/50; `Profilerhtml.changelog.md` 49/50, untouched by a Phase 4 row
- **THE DEPLOY WAS MEASURED, NOT FORECAST, AND THE ONE-LINE FORECAST HELD EXACTLY.** One `.gs` in the merge diff fired exactly one step and logged exactly one line, on the **first GET leg** with no POLL and no `?op=deploy` probe: `Classroom deploy confirmed (GET): Updated to v01.49g (deployment 60) | 60/200`. Pages (`gs-versions/Classroomgs.version.txt`) read `|v01.49g|` and agreed, so the §7.19 order resolved on its first two reads — **its seventeenth consecutive clean use**. Every other project's step exited 0 without firing and logged nothing, so **Scraper holds at 155/200** exactly as §7.34 predicted. *(Recorded at v06.06r — counters can only be measured after the merge.)*
- **Checkers.** `check-classroom-content.py` **0 errors / 0 warnings at 44 lessons, 8 tracks, 142 gate cases**, the guidance-module assertion **still 18** as a Phase 4 row requires; the gate-case count did **not** move, because an all-public stamp reuses fixture classes the truth table already covers. `check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict` exit 0, no structural findings, **28 stale pins** (unchanged — this lesson's eight pins are all current) and **2 items due for review**, both by design. `check-classroom-pipeline.py --selftest` **13 fixtures / 0 failures**. `node --check` clean; `check-gas-inner-scripts.js` 9 files / 86 blocks clean; `check-readme-tree.py` 0 findings after `--fix` synced the one drifted GAS display

- **Two heredoc accidents worth recording, because the second repeated the first.** Writing a long changelog section through an **unquoted** bash heredoc lets the shell expand every backticked span as a command substitution, so ``` `Sections: 105/100` ``` and every `file.md` reference vanish from the text. It happened once on the v06.03r section (caught by the counter still reading 104 and no section added) and again here (caught by reading the written section back). **The fix both times: write the section to a file with a QUOTED heredoc and substitute the timestamp in Python.** No checker watches changelog prose for shell damage
- **Nothing in a deployed surface moved**, so no GAS bump, no page bump and no public changelog entry

### Notes
- **§10.6 (t) fired on FOUR WHOLE SECTIONS OF TWO PUBLIC LESSONS.** This is the first segment **two of whose three `READ_NEXT` mechanism lessons are already built**, and between them `bridge-power` and `backup-generation` teach **five of the six buying criteria** — the four machine classes, the slot-reservation instrument, the standby/prime/continuous ladder, block load and the whole permit ceiling — **naming not one vendor**. Earlier pre-declarations were one tile (session 6) or one callout (session 8). §7.23's test held on **the slot itself**: a mechanism explaining why the escape routes exist there, a balance-sheet position a permit-less buyer can own here
- **THE FINDING: `bridge-power` states there are exactly THREE ways out of a factory queue and names nobody — and the registry's five challengers fill all three ONE-FOR-ONE.** Machine class → `rolls-royce-power-systems`, `rehlko`; provenance → `proenergy`; a machine nobody queues for → `bloom-energy`, `mainspring-energy`. **The first time a public lesson's own CLASSIFICATION has mapped one-to-one onto a segment's challenger bench**, which generalises session 6's (s) from *the lesson teaches the test* to *the lesson teaches the categories and the module fills them*
- **SIX built landscape neighbours is a record, and the inversion rate is BIMODAL.** `in-hall-power` 6 shared / **6 inverted (100 %)**; `grid-equipment` 3 shared / **0 inverted (0 %)**; plus `storage-developers-and-ipps` 3/3, `storage-integrators-and-containers` 6/4, `utilities` 2/1, `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` 2/1. **The rate measures whether a shared member's business CROSSES the boundary or STRADDLES it** — a finer instrument than session 8's direction. **Twenty-one omissions** across seven neighbours are enumerated in the header comment and in §2g of the analysis file
- **The module's own judgment**, labelled as analysis wherever it appears: incumbency here is a claim on **delivery** rather than on manufacturing — **63 of 116 GW committed is reservations, not orders**, and **two of the eight incumbents manufacture no prime mover at all** — and the challengers are **renting a window the incumbents are closing with capital rather than price**, which an incumbent states against itself
- **`reviewBy` 2026-12-31 — session 3's (g) case a THIRD time, and the one future date in the fence is a TRAP rather than an absence.** 60 `policyExposure[]` entries across seventeen members, 24 dated, **exactly one in the future**: `cummins`'s `2027-01`, whose own text says it affects the **on-highway** platforms rather than the data-centre gen-sets. A mechanical sort returns it and clocks a bridge-generation module **on a truck engine**. The date taken was read out of `caterpillar`'s `strategyRead[5]` and **corroborated by four other members converging on end-2026** — the first landscape gate resting on a convergence rather than on one sentence. **Four rejections in writing**, and **the first deliberate acceptance of a calendar day another module already carries** (`china-policy-stack-2026-08`): (r)/(u) reject another module's own *gate*, and this is a different bell on the same day; shifting to 30 December would mean fabricating a date the corpus does not state
- **`section 232` DECIDED rather than deferred a third time (§7.32 item vi).** Session 8's ground — *a tariff regime belonging with the policy seeds* — **does not survive inspection: no policy seed owns it.** `topic-china-policy` holds `tariff` and `Section 301` but is China-scoped, and 232 is a steel-and-aluminium-derivatives statute biting Germany, France and Japan; four members here carry that exposure and two name the regime in their own `policyExposure[]`. It stays out on the **sharpened** ground that it is a **cross-segment** regime and a landscape session may only add `topic-landscape-<segment>`. Recommendation recorded: it wants its own `topic-trade-tariffs` seed or an addition to `topic-federal-action`
- **§8 item 8's `bridge-power` → `where-batteries-stop` pointer: REPORTED, not fixed — an S2 session does not edit lessons.** `bridge-power` was read in full. **The debt is real in the letter and paid in substance**: `where-batteries-stop` states the boundary **twice**, names `bridge-power` by title both times and declares the non-duplication in both directions; `bridge-power` carries no back-pointer and its only cross-reference is forward, to the transformer lesson. **Recommendation: close the open half as paid, and add the back-pointer only inside a substantive revision of `bridge-power` — never as a standalone edit, which is the G4 change P8 exists to fail**

Three sub-findings carry it: a **virtual PPA hands volume risk to the buyer from an unexpected direction**, because it settles on what the project generated rather than on what the buyer consumed; a **take-or-pay is not a promise to be paid in full** — one disclosed shortfall make-whole is estimated at fifty to sixty-five per cent of full impact and then capped, and the cap is where the risk transfer stops; and **credit enhancement has an observable price quoted in the coupon**, with three landlords paying nothing, ~5.4 % of themselves and ~14 % for the same guarantor backstopping the same tenant, against an unenhanced issue ~370 bp wider

### Notes

- **THE DEPLOY CORRECTED A RULE THREE BRIEFS HAD CARRIED, and arithmetic rather than log-reading is what caught it.** Both legs fired — `Scraper deploy confirmed (GET): Updated to v02.07g (deployment 152) | 152/200` and `Classroom deploy confirmed (GET): Updated to v01.42g (deployment 53) | 53/200` — each on the **first GET leg with no POLL line and no `?op=deploy` probe issued**, the **tenth consecutive** clean use of the §7.19 order and the first two-line deploy since v05.83r. Pages confirmed `|v01.42g|` and `|v02.07g|` before the job log was opened, so the two sources agreed and no probe was warranted
- **Classroom's counter was exact at 53/200. Scraper's came in ONE ABOVE the 151/200 forecast.** Read the chain back to the last measured anchor: **150/200 at v05.78r** (`Updated to v02.05g (deployment 150)`) → v05.83r's leg, poll-resolved as `Already up to date (v02.06g)` and recorded as consuming none → v05.85r's step never fired and logged nothing → **152/200 here**. This push's `Updated to` accounts for exactly one. **One consumption is unaccounted for, and the only Scraper deploy activity in the interval was v05.83r's**
- **So §7.26's middle case is wrong as stated, and the mechanism is recoverable.** `Already up to date` is what the deployment reports at **read** time — it is not a statement that the call spent nothing. v05.83r's first GET leg **failed to confirm and had already run `pullAndDeployFromGitHub()`**, consuming a deployment; the poll then re-read a bundle that was by then current and printed `Already up to date`. The v05.84r conclusion was inferred from the log's wording and was never measured across the event. **This is the first measurement that spans it.** The corrected rule, stated as the inference it is: `Updated to` consumes one; a step that never fires consumes none and logs nothing; **`Already up to date` is silent on consumption and must not be forecast as zero**
- **The transferable lesson is about evidence class, not about deployments.** A rule derived from *what a log says* survives until someone does *arithmetic across two measurements*. Three consecutive briefs restated the middle case because each read one run's wording; none subtracted an anchor. **When a counter is the thing being forecast, anchor the forecast on the last measured value and reconcile the whole interval — a per-run reading cannot detect a silent consumption**

- **THE FINDING OF THE SESSION: a landscape can have to split against a public LESSON, and that split crosses an ACCESS TIER rather than only a subject.** This segment has **four** neighbours against session 5's three — `large-load-interconnection-2026-09`, `utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08`, `power-infra-aidc-2026-08`, and the Phase 4 row 9 mechanism lesson `the-campus-as-a-power-project`, which is **public** and built on nine of this segment's own members. A tile reads **18 of 30**
- **The second and third judgments.** The threat is **not share loss — the scarce asset moved, from land to an energised interconnection**, which is why seven of thirteen challengers are converted miners competing on a date rather than on a product. ### Notes

- **THE DEPLOY, and it produced a third case of the counter rule.** §7.26 established that `Already up to date` consumes no deployment where `Updated to` consumes one. v05.85r's run produced **neither on the Scraper leg, because the Scraper deploy step never fired**: each project's step opens with `git diff --name-only "$PRE" HEAD | grep -q "$GS" || exit 0`, and `Scraper.gs` was not in the merge diff, so the step exited 0 in under a second without contacting the deployment at all. **So the rule has three cases and only the first spends anything** — `Updated to` consumes one; `Already up to date` consumes none because the leg ran and found the bundle current; and a step that never fires consumes none *and logs nothing*. Forecasting from "whether the running bundle is behind" gets the number right in all three but describes only the middle case: **to predict the log line you will actually read, ask first whether the file is in the diff**
- **Classroom confirmed on the first GET leg with no POLL and no probe** — `Classroom deploy confirmed (GET): Updated to v01.41g (deployment 52) | 52/200` — the **ninth consecutive** clean use of the §7.19 order. - **The lesson's argument**, stated so a later revision can test it: a campus is **three clocks, not one** — the developer's, which money and crews can compress almost arbitrarily; the utility's, which they cannot move by a day; and the tenant's, which a signature starts — and a campus is finished when the slowest of them finishes. The five-rung ladder (land, entitled, powered, leased, operating) is what a published gigawatt figure is silently counted at; the four-question announcement test is *how many megawatts, measured how, at which rung, on whose clock*; and the five failure modes are all cases where the megawatts were never the campus's to promise

### Notes

- **THE FINDING OF THIS RUN, and it is a new class: a shallow clone silently falsifies `git log` pins, and `--check` is the alarm that rings.** The session opened with `build-classroom-segments.py --check` reporting **19 due** against the brief's measured 5, every one of the fourteen extra saying `inputs moved: concepts:profiler-concepts 2026-09-13→2026-09-14` with `sections differing: none`. The cause is not the registry: **the fresh container's clone is shallow (51 commits), and its boundary commit `75a4314` appears to introduce every file in the repo**, so `git log -1 --format=%cs -- <path>` stops at the boundary and returns *its* date rather than the file's. `git fetch --unshallow origin main` (1,216 commits) restored the true answer — `5eab070`, **2026-09-13** — and `--check` fell straight back to **5 due**, the exact five the brief named. **The cost of not noticing would not have been the due count.** §8 item 2 and G2 require `concepts:` and `project:` pins to be read from `git log`, so authoring off the shallow clone would have written `concepts:profiler-concepts@2026-09-14` into the stamp when the true pin is 2026-09-13 — a silent freshness bug of exactly the kind the rule exists to prevent, invisible to every checker, and it would have set a false baseline for the next C2 refresh. §7.24 named unshallowing only as the precondition for CHANGELOG SHA enrichment; **it is equally a precondition for the pin reads, and on a fresh container it must be done before any pin is taken.** §7.25 states it that way
- **The brief's input count was one short, and the contract is the arithmetic.** §7.24 says "twenty-nine inputs … eighteen `study:` guides". §3.4's base list carries eight `study:` refs and its v05.07r amendment adds eleven, so the roster is **nineteen study guides and thirty candidate inputs**. All thirty were fetched and **all thirty were drawn on and pinned** — none dropped
- **The omission record therefore moves one level down, as row 7's did, and this time it has a single structural reason.** Nothing was fetched-and-not-pinned at the *input* level; what was fetched and not drawn on is a large set of *sections*, and the overwhelming majority are **capital structure and accounting** — the ABS and master-trust sections, the convertible-note and capped-call machinery, the REIT metrics (FFO, cap rate, mark-to-market, renewal spreads), the money-ladder bars, the sponsor and GP-fund structures, the collateral tables, the consolidation and equity-method treatments. §3.5 assigns that material to `contracts-and-revenue` (row 11) and `reading-the-numbers` (row 25), so it is not this lesson's to teach. Three named carve-outs beside it: **cooling** (`pue-wue-and-the-cooling-stack`, `cooling-water-and-the-density-transition`, `density-and-cooling-in-the-lease`, and the PUE/free-cooling half of `why-cold-windy-nowhere-wins`) belongs to §2.2's one-lesson cap and row 18; **turbine and reactor machinery** (`what-a-gas-turbine-is`, `simple-cycle-first-combined-cycle-later`, `the-nuclear-application`) to rows 3 and 16; and **bitcoin-mining economics** (`difficulty-ratchet`, `the-halving`, `hashprice`, `efficiency-and-cost`) to no lesson at all — only the conversion table and the stranding argument were taken from that guide
- **The due-count prediction was measured and it held exactly: `--check` went 5 due → 8 due.** The three newly due are `clean-firm-and-nuclear`, `epc-and-construction` and `hyperscalers-and-ai-labs`; `aidc-developers-and-landlords` and `neoclouds` were already in the standing five and gained a second reason. All eight report `inputs moved: none`, and seven differ in `read-next` alone — the bound row 8 established (|`READ_NEXT` membership| + prior residue) held for the second consecutive row. **The predicted title mismatch also appeared as described**: `PLANNED_TITLES["the-campus-as-a-power-project"]` reads *Reading the Eight Named Projects* against the authored *Reading the Named Projects*, so each diff is the title string as well as the `" (planned)"` marker — expected, not a defect. **Nothing was regenerated**: `segment-*` literals are S2's under §10.9, and `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` is §7.25's own residue to clear
- **`reviewBy` took the six-month default (2027-03-15) with three candidates rejected in writing**, on the test rows 7 and 8 set. (i) A campus lease stated as binding but "effective" only on closing **by 30 September 2026** with financing and board approvals — a closing condition on one deal, not a rule taking effect, and this lesson teaches the generation-first *model* rather than that lease. (ii) A lender's covenant stepping up amortisation if no lease of at least 400 MW exists **by November 2026** — a date inside one company's capital stack, which no section of this lesson leans a claim on. (iii) Two campus delivery dates (**mid-2029**, and shells running **June 2026 – September 2027**) — project schedules, and the lesson's own claim is that such schedules slip, so a date moving *confirms* the claim rather than contradicting it. The rules the lesson does teach are already in force (the 2025 curtailment statute, the 2026 large-load tariff and its deposit rule), so there is no future gate to pin to
- **The six-column table rendered clean at 1400px, and it is the widest any mechanism lesson has carried by column count.** `the-named-projects` is **6 × 9** against row 6's 5 × 11 and row 7's 5 × 6, with no overflow and no horizontal scroll. That is a **tenth clean data point** for the open `.cl-tbl` `min-width` question and the first at six columns, which settles column count as the cause about as firmly as evidence can — the v05.57r defect remains a first column holding one long unbreakable word. **The item still stays recorded-not-taken**, because closing it means editing `Classroom.html` and this row only read it
- **A recorded item was investigated and reassigned rather than deferred again — and the reassignment is the useful half.** §7.24 offered `the-800-vdc-shift`'s three-inserts-stale `three-chains` intro to this row on the grounds that it "sits in Phase 4 row 9's own track". **It does not.** `the-800-vdc-shift` is in `aidc-grid-to-chip` (position 9) and this lesson went into `aidc-campus` (position 3), so **this row's insertion does not move that lesson's predecessor by a single position** and row 9 has no more claim on it than rows 7 and 8 had. The boundary was last moved by rows 7 and 8 and will not move again. **So it is not waiting for a row with a claim; it belongs to whichever session opens that lesson for its own reason — a G3 revision when its inputs move, or a developer sweep — and §7.25 records it that way** so it stops being re-offered to each successive row

- **The segment re-measured on the day: 14 members, 6 · 2 · 6** — §7.23's figure confirmed **exactly**, the second S2 session running to confirm rather than correct. Position 15 of 19, tier `demand`, six buying criteria, floor met with room
- **`reviewBy` is 2026-10-01 — seventeen days out, and the module therefore ships inside its own 30-day review horizon.** The 14 members carry **76 `policyExposure[]` entries, 64 dated, and exactly one of those dates is in the future** (2027-01-01, Dominion's GS-5), so a sort of the fence returns 2027-01-01. The nearer gate was **read out of `southern-company`'s policy prose** — an Alabama 150 MW threshold statute effective 1 October 2026 — while that same entry's `effectiveDate` field carries the earlier Georgia step. **Session 2's read-don't-sort finding fires for the third time, and for the first time it produces an answer *nearer* than the sort rather than differently shaped.** Four candidates rejected in writing: three hearings and an election (not rules taking effect), the 10 December 2026 verification report (a deliverable inside an audit — and the date the procurement module's own calendar leads with), Dominion's ~29 October line-extension policy (a date computed from a ninety-day clock, which is a window by another name), and 2027-01-01 itself. `check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict` now reports **1 item due for review** and still exits 0 with no structural findings — review dates never call `strict()`
- **The module's thesis: in this segment market share is fixed by statute, so nothing can be ranked but the instrument.** Six regulated franchises, none competing with another; five instruments across them (the filter, the certificate in two variants, the contract, the collateral, the customer-specific charge). The two `challenger`s are **larger by revenue than four of the six incumbents** — NRG first at USD 30,713M and Vistra third at 17,738M — because the registry's own `notes` says regulated franchises have no challenger by construction and the role is carried by competitive retail suppliers. **`challenger` here is a structural placeholder, not a threat claim**, and the module says so rather than padding a threat section
- **The sharpest fact the segment produces: in ERCOT the challengers are the incumbent's customers.** Oncor's own `productsAndServices › targetSegments` states that Vistra's and NRG's retail subsidiaries are its two largest customers at **25 per cent and 21 per cent of 2025 operating revenues**, with no other customer above 10 per cent — 46 per cent of the largest pure-play wires incumbent's revenue, corroborated independently by all three dossiers, and typed `other` rather than `competitor` in the graph
- **The threat is structural rather than competitive, and the six adjacents carry all three routes** — the load never becomes the franchise's load (co-location and behind-the-meter; the August 2026 Texas pause **exempted** behind-the-meter generation and caught grid-served load); the asset arrives already built (build-own-transfer gas plants, utility-owned slices of merchant lines); or the franchise changes hands (the USD 67B NextEra combination, which Dominion's own strategy read says "changes the buyer more than the docket")
- **The split with `utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08` is the session's structural obligation and is stated in the module's opening paragraph.** That module owns the **process** — the four moves, the tariff-terms table, the five BESS channels, the buyer map, the calendar. This one owns the **parties** — the instrument, the eight bets, the three routes, and Xcel plus both challengers plus all six adjacents, none of which the playbook carries. The module deliberately carries **no** tariff table, **no** channel list and **no** buyer map, and its `check-yourself` closes on the split so a reader cannot miss it. The one shared number (AEP Ohio's 30 GW → 5.6 GW) is cited here from AEP's own PUCO figure (17,861 MW against 30,000 MW) and does a different job

### Notes

- **The brief's due-count prediction was right in range and wrong in mechanism, and the mechanism is the finding.** `build-classroom-segments.py --check` went **2 due → 5 due**, inside the predicted 5–7, but not for the reason the brief gave. §7.22 described **two independent channels** — `READ_NEXT` membership and the buying-criteria lexicon — and named `grid-equipment` and `hyperscalers-and-ai-labs` as lexicon candidates because both carry a criterion containing the word "availability". Neither went due, and **neither ever could have**: `sec_what_is_bought` in `scripts/build-classroom-segments.py` guards its lexicon match with `if lid in eligible`, where `eligible = set(READ_NEXT.get(seg["id"], []))`. **The lexicon is not a second channel; it is subordinate to `READ_NEXT`.** A new lesson can only alter a segment whose `READ_NEXT` already names it, and the lexicon decides only whether such a segment differs in one section or two. The five due are `in-hall-power` (**two** sections — `read-next` plus `what-is-bought-and-on-what`, because its criterion reads "installed MW per protected MW under the chosen redundancy topology" and hits `"redundan"`), `aidc-developers-and-landlords` and `neoclouds` (`read-next` only), plus `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` and `compute-and-the-rack` carried from row 7. **The bound for every future Phase 4 row is |READ_NEXT membership| + prior residue**, which is a checkable number rather than a range. `PLANNED_TITLES["redundancy-by-the-numbers"]` was already `"N+1, 2N, and Six Nines"`, byte-identical to the authored title, so the whole diff on each segment is a `" (planned)"` marker disappearing. **Nothing was regenerated** — §10.9 gives `segment-*` literals to S2.
- **`reviewBy` 2027-03-14 on the six-month default, with both candidates rejected in writing.** A scan of all ten study guides for future-dated material returned exactly two hits. The first is a nuclear deployment milestone stated as *"targeted for late 2027"* — a **window rather than a date**, and, more decisively, on a topic **no section of this lesson teaches**; that is session 1's Cummins test and S2 session 2's "late 2026" test applied together. The second is one campus's construction clock, whose only forward entries are a 2027 workforce peak and a 2028 completion — **a project schedule, not a rule taking effect**, and the same class of thing row 7 rejected as "projected from ~2027". Nothing in the fetched material carries a dated gate, so the default stands.
- **The content contract held on material that is half construction-management.** No flashcard answer and no quiz correct choice is a proper noun, and — the stronger test row 7 set — **no company or product name appears anywhere in the rendered body**. The self-declared-tier discussion is taught as "one published guide to exactly this practice" and "the self-declaring operator", the nines are attributed to "one hyperscale operator's own teaching" and "a large general contractor's reliability practice", so every name lives only in `provenance.inputs[]`, which the renderer never formats.
- **A source contradiction was taught rather than smoothed over.** One fetched guide reports, inside a single document, an industry expectation of roughly ninety-six hours of on-site ride-through at the top certified Uptime rung **and** the self-declaring operator's own framing of that same rung as a twelve-hour benchmark its hundred-hour standard beats. Both sentences are about the same rung and neither is audited. Rather than pick one figure or drop both, `the-tiers` teaches the gap as the point — *the benchmark a self-declared standard beats is chosen by the party declaring it* — which is the guide's own stated lesson that a self-declared standard "can change definitions", made concrete.

- **A structural false positive in the stop hook, diagnosed rather than worked around.** At the close of v05.74r the hook reported "1 unpushed commit". It was not one: `f9ebfdf` is an ancestor of `origin/main` and `rev-list origin/main..HEAD` was 0. The hook resolves `upstream` to `origin/$current_branch` when that ref *resolves locally* — and `origin/claude/hopeful-gates-1lzznw` still resolved as a **stale remote-tracking ref**, because `git fetch origin main` is refspec-limited and prunes nothing outside `main`. The commit it counted was **the workflow's own `Update last-processed-commit.sha … [skip ci]`**, already on the remote, pulled in by `git pull --ff-only origin main`. **`git fetch --prune origin` clears it** and the upstream then falls back to `origin/HEAD`. Re-pushing would have been the wrong fix — it would queue a second workflow run on already-merged commits, which the flow's own stale-branch guard exists to reject. This will recur on every session that follows the deployment flow; the one-line inoculation is to make the post-merge sync `git fetch --prune origin`.
- **A live branch was nearly mistaken for a stale ref.** `origin/claude/adoring-brown-mvddj2` survived `--prune`; `git ls-remote` shows it **exists on the remote and is not merged into main** — a real branch from a different session. Left untouched.
- **Rotation checked on the EST clock, not the UTC one.** 115 sections with this entry, **17 dated 2026-09-14 and therefore exempt → 98 non-exempt → no rotation.** `Scrapergs.changelog.md` stays **51 raw / 49 non-exempt** and remains armed for the next session to touch `Scraper.gs` on a later EST day; `Classroomgs.changelog.md` 36 / 29; `Profilerhtml.changelog.md` 49 / 49.

## [v05.68r] — 2026-09-14 02:16:52 PM EST

> **Prompt:** (continuation of the v05.67r session — the deploy-confirmation probe's own cost, measured after the push landed and corrected in the brief it would otherwise have been repeated in.)

**A one-paragraph correction to §7.19, made because the instruction it was about to hand the next session had just cost a deployment version here.** v05.67r merged and deployed green, and its independent confirmation — issued exactly as every brief since v05.59r has specified, **after** the Deploy step finished — was itself a second deploy.

### Changed

- **`INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.19 — the deploy paragraph rewritten, and the counter corrected from 42/200 to 44/200.** The workflow's own step reported `Classroom deploy confirmed (GET): Updated to v01.33g (deployment 43) | 43/200` on the **first GET leg with no `POLL <n>` line** — the v05.59r poll is armed and has still never been needed. The confirmation probe several minutes later answered `Updated to v01.33g (deployment 44) | 44/200`: not a confirmation, a deploy
- **The mechanism, read out of `pullAndDeployFromGitHub()` rather than guessed.** It fetches `Classroom.gs` from GitHub, reads the pulled `VERSION`, and compares it against the `VERSION` constant **of the code currently executing**. Equal returns `Already up to date (…)` and does nothing; unequal **deploys**. Between a deployment being repointed and `/exec` actually executing the new bundle there is a propagation lag, so a probe inside that window runs under the *old* `VERSION`, sees a mismatch, and deploys again. "After the Deploy step has finished" is therefore **necessary and not sufficient** — the real condition is that `/exec` is already running the new `VERSION`
- **The order that costs nothing, now written into §7.19:** read Pages (`gs-versions/Classroomgs.version.txt` is a static file, free to poll) and the workflow's own `Classroom deploy confirmed (GET)` line from the job log. Probe `?op=deploy` **only if those two disagree**, accept that it may deploy once, and re-probe afterwards — a second probe returning `Already up to date (v01.33g)` is how the settled state was established here, and it consumed no version

### Notes

- **Final live state, confirmed twice:** GAS `Already up to date (v01.33g)`, Pages serving `|v01.33g|` and `|v01.14w|`, workflow run **green**, branch swept. Deployment counter **44/200**
- **Why this is a second commit and not an amendment:** v05.67r is physically on `main`, which is the one case the single-commit-per-interaction rule names as unavoidable
- **Rotation checked, not assumed:** 108 sections with this one, of which **10 are dated 2026-09-14 and therefore exempt → 98 non-exempt → no rotation.** No page or GAS version moves — this touches `README.md`, `repository.version.txt`, one plan document and this file

## [v05.59r] — 2026-09-14 03:23:58 AM EST

> **Prompt:** "continue with your recommendation"

**The deploy gate stops going red on a deploy that worked.** v05.58r diagnosed run #568 and corrected the briefs; this closes the hole in the mechanism itself. `.github/scripts/gas-deploy.sh` had exactly two chances to read a confirmation — one GET, one POST — and no third. It now has seven.

### Fixed

- **`.github/scripts/gas-deploy.sh`** — after the GET and POST legs both fail to confirm, the script re-reads the GET route **five more times** behind 5/15/30/60/90s of backoff at `--max-time 45`, reporting the confirming leg as `POLL <n>` so a run log shows the retry was needed. **The evidence this is the right fix, rather than a longer timeout:** run #568's whole step took **76 seconds**, so neither leg came near its 120s ceiling — the exec endpoint answered with something that was not the function's return string. Reproduced by hand minutes later: one GET returned Google's HTML shell, and the very next GET returned `Already up to date (v01.29g)`. The failure is a **non-answer**, not a slow deploy, so a re-read is what fixes it and a raised timeout would not have
- Re-reading is safe and idempotent by construction: the GET route runs `pullAndDeployFromGitHub()` — the same call the first leg made — and once the version matches it returns `Already up to date (<v>)` without acting. A poll therefore either catches a deploy that landed or retries one that did not

### Changed

- **`.claude/rules/workflows.md`** → "GAS Self-Update Deploy Verification" gains the poll, the run #568 evidence, and the consequence that made this worth fixing: `deploy` is gated `needs.auto-merge.result == 'success'`, so **a red GAS gate skips the GitHub Pages deploy entirely** — on #568 the GAS project served the new lesson while the live site still served the previous `gs-versions/*.txt` and `gs-changelogs/*.md`. The `workflow_dispatch`-on-`main` recovery is documented beside it

### Notes

- **The deferred-failure contract is untouched, and was tested rather than assumed.** `gas-deploy.sh` still exits **0** on failure and still records to `$RUNNER_TEMP/gas-deploy-failures`, so `Delete branch` and `Sweep stale claude branches` keep running and the final `Fail the run if any GAS deploy was unconfirmed` step is what turns the run red. Verified with a stubbed `curl` and `sleep` on `PATH` across six scenarios: confirm on GET (1 call), confirm on POST (2 calls), confirm on poll 2 (4 calls, later polls not attempted), never confirms (7 calls, exit 0, FAILFILE written, error names the polls), the ≥170/200 version-ceiling warning still firing, and the no-`VERSION` guard still refusing to report an unverifiable deploy
- **What this commit cannot prove, stated plainly.** Each `Deploy <Project>` step exits early unless that project's `.gs` appears in the merge diff, and this commit touches no `.gs`. So its own workflow run does **not** exercise the new code — the first real test is the next push that changes a GAS project, which is Phase 4 row 5. The expected signal there is either a clean first-leg confirmation as usual, or a `POLL <n>` line showing the retry earned its place
- **The script itself is not lengthened where it matters.** The success path is unchanged: a project that confirms on the first GET pays nothing. The added cost is ~7 minutes worst case and lands only on a project that never confirms — which is the case that is currently costing a red run and a skipped Pages deploy
- **Archive rotation fired for the first time in seven pushes.** EST rolled to 2026-09-14, so the fourteen sections dated 2026-09-13 stopped being exempt and the non-exempt count went 96 → 111. The oldest whole date group — **thirteen sections dated 2026-09-04** — moved to `CHANGELOG-archive.md` with SHA enrichment on every header, leaving 98 non-exempt and `Sections: 99/100`

## [v05.58r] — 2026-09-13 09:34:44 PM EST

> **Prompt:** "continue with your recommendation"

**A correction to a claim this session wrote an hour earlier and then disproved.** The v05.57r push merged cleanly and its workflow run went **red** at `Fail the run if any GAS deploy was unconfirmed` — and the deploy had in fact landed. The paste-in briefs in §7.14 and §7.15 both told the next session that a red gate "means the deploy did not land". It does not, and a session acting on that would have concluded its own lesson was not live.

### Fixed

- **`INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.14 and §7.15** — the deploy-verification paragraph now states what run #568 actually showed. `.github/scripts/gas-deploy.sh` fires **one** GET (`--max-time 120`) and, failing that, **one** POST, with no poll afterwards; when the GET leg outruns its budget the confirmation is lost even though Apps Script completes the update seconds later. A direct `curl` two minutes after the run returned `Already up to date (v01.29g)`. `Classroom.gs` is now ~1.9 MB and is the slowest project in the repo, so it is the one that hits this — and it grows by roughly 450 lines per lesson with twenty-two lesson rows still to run

### Notes

- **The consequence worth knowing, and the reason this is not cosmetic.** The `deploy` job is gated `needs.auto-merge.result == 'success'`, so a red GAS gate **skips the GitHub Pages deploy entirely**. On run #568 the GAS project was current while the live site still served the previous `Classroomgs.version.txt` and `Classroomgs.changelog.md` — the version pill and the changelog popup were stale against a lesson that was already being served
- **Recovery, now documented in both briefs.** A `workflow_dispatch` on `main`: `auto-merge` is gated `github.ref != 'refs/heads/main'` so it skips, and `deploy` fires through its `auto-merge.result == 'skipped' && is-initialized` branch. Run **#569** did exactly that and went green — `deploy` and `mirror-library` both success, Pages now serving `|v01.29g|`
- **The deploy script itself is deliberately not changed here.** A poll after the POST leg would close this properly — `Already up to date (<v>)` is the idempotent confirmation and is precisely what a later GET returns — but that path confirms all eight GAS projects, its deferred-failure design is explicitly flagged in the briefs as something a session must not "tidy up", and verifying a change to it means watching a real merge. It is the developer's call, and it is the recommendation left at the close of this session
- No GAS or page version bump: no deployed content changed ([PC-GS-VERSION] #1), so no page or GAS changelog entry

## [v05.56r] — 2026-09-13 08:43:31 PM EST

> **Prompt:** "continue with your recommendation"

**The defect v05.55r found by eye is now a checker that cannot miss it.** Nothing in this repo could see it: the schema was valid, the terms resolved, `node --check` saw good JSON, and the pipeline checker reads diffs. It was caught in a screenshot, which does not scale. This closes that gap — and the implementation is narrower than the recommendation that prompted it, for reasons worth recording.

### Added

- **`check_markup()` in `scripts/check-classroom-content.py`** — simulates the two substitutions `clFmt` applies, in its order, over every string the renderer actually formats, and **errors** on any surviving `*`, naming the section id and the exact path (`section 'what-a-battery-changes' cards[1].adv[0]`). Covers all 33 lessons, 8 tracks and, separately, the nine `guidanceDoc<Name>_()` literals below the `// CONTENT END` fence — those are not lessons and nothing else in this checker validates them, but `Classroom.html` renders them through the same `cl*` engine, so they carry the identical hazard. 108 module sections scanned, all clean

### Changed

- **`CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` → Verification** and **`.claude/rules/classroom-app.md` → Versioning and verification** now state the rule — *one level of emphasis per span* — and the two scope properties that keep it honest
- **`INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.14** — its manual simulation recipe is retired in favour of the checker, and the paragraph now records that the recipe it carried named **too wide a scope**

### Notes

- **The recommendation this session acted on was partly wrong, and reading the renderer is what caught it.** v05.55r proposed checking `sections`, `tiles`, `glossary`, `short` and `title`. Three of those five are wrong: `tiles`, `glossary`, the lesson's `title`/`short`, section `title`/`read`, proscons card `t`/`meta` and timeline lane labels all reach the DOM through **`clEl()`'s `textContent`**, where an asterisk is a literal character. Checking them would have manufactured a fresh class of false positives on the very defect class being guarded. The implemented scope is the sixteen clFmt call sites in `Classroom.html`, read off the file rather than assumed
- **The walk had to be kind-aware, which the recommendation did not anticipate.** A `timeline` item's `label` and `sub` go through `clFmt`; a **`bars` item's `label` and `sub` do not** — `clBars` uses `createTextNode` and `textContent`. Identical field names, opposite answers, so `FMT_BY_KIND` is keyed on `kind` and mirrors the renderer call site by call site. Proven directly: the same nested-emphasis string in both shapes yields **0 errors as `bars`, 2 as `timeline`**
- **`provenance.inputs[].note` is excluded by construction**, because the schema says a note is an authoring aid that is never rendered. That is the `P=V*I` in `cell-to-container` — a multiplication sign that v05.55r's first, wider sweep flagged and then cleared. The narrow scope makes that class of false positive impossible rather than merely unlikely
- **The check was proven to fire, not merely to pass.** A checker that never fires is worthless, so the real v05.55r defect string was re-injected into a scratch clone and caught with its exact path; a nested emphasis injected into `guidanceDocBankability_()`'s `ps[0]` was caught with its module and section. In both negative tests the error count went **24 → 25**, confirming the injected provenance-note asterisk in the same run produced nothing
- **No GAS version bump, and that is the rule rather than an omission.** `Classroom.gs` is untouched this push, so [PC-GS-VERSION] #1 forbids bumping `VERSION`, and there is no page or GAS changelog entry because no deployed content changed. The README's Classroom GAS display correctly stays at `v01.28g`
- **`check-classroom-content.py`: 33 lesson(s), 8 track(s), 142 gate case(s) — 24 error(s), 0 warning(s)**, error set diffed line by line against a pristine `HEAD` worktree and byte-identical — the new check adds nothing to the baseline every future session compares against. `check-classroom-curriculum --strict`: no structural findings. `check-classroom-pipeline --selftest` 13/0; `--base origin/main` reports **P1 alone** — no P5 (no registry touched) and no P3 (no gate surface touched), which is the right signature for a checker-and-docs change

### Notes

- **A silent rendering defect was found, and it was this session's own.** `clFmt` in `Classroom.html` resolves bold with `/\*\*([^*]+)\*\*/g` — a character class that **cannot contain an asterisk** — so *italic nested inside bold* never matches, the italic pass then chews the string, and **literal asterisks render to the reader**. Two strings in the `what-a-battery-changes` proscons hit it. Nothing catches this: `check-classroom-content.py` validates schema and terms, the pipeline checker reads diffs, and `node --check` sees valid JSON. **It was visible only in the screenshot.** Both were rewritten to use one level of emphasis, and the two regexes were then simulated over every rendered string in **all 33 lessons and 8 tracks** — `sections`, `tiles`, `glossary`, `short`, `title` — which now leaves **zero** surviving asterisks corpus-wide. The hazard is written into §7.14 so the next author checks it before pushing rather than after
- **The sweep's one other hit was a false positive, and the distinction matters.** `cell-to-container` appeared to have a broken string until it was traced to `provenance.inputs[1].note` — `"P=V*I, why plants run high DC voltage…"`. The schema says a `note` is an *authoring aid, never rendered*, so the asterisk there is a multiplication sign that never reaches `clFmt`. Scoping the sweep to rendered surfaces only is what separated a real defect from an arithmetic symbol; a checker built on this should inherit that scoping
- **P5 fired on a `lessons[]` insertion and the finding is a real consequence, not noise.** The checker's message is precise — *"a reorder moves every account's study-next pointer without a tick changing"* — and `clStudyNext_`, run against the real PROJECT region, confirms it: an account that had completed all four previously-built lessons of `aidc-grid-to-chip` is now pointed back at `backup-generation`. For a newly published lesson that is the intended behaviour and the reason §4 tells a developer session to insert at position rather than append; for an unattended run it is forbidden, which is why P5 exists. **No P3** — `gateDigest` correctly untouched
- **The spec and §8 item 6 disagree, and the spec won.** Item 6 asserts that no outline runs two `prose` sections back to back. §3.3's outline does — `block-load-and-the-swinging-hall` then `paralleling-and-load-shed` — and the ids and order are load-bearing (progress keys and drill ids), so they were followed exactly and the inaccuracy recorded in the §7 row rather than papered over by changing a kind
- **The two `profile:` stamps are archetypes, not company trivia.** They appear once, in `the-permit-is-the-ceiling`, as *shapes* of machine — the gas-microgrid platform and the linear generator whose sub-1.5 ppm NOx without after-treatment is a permitting wedge — and no drill item asks about either. That is the mechanism-lesson content contract observed at the point it is easiest to breach
- **`check-classroom-content.py`: 33 lesson(s), 8 track(s), 142 gate case(s) — 24 error(s), 0 warning(s)**, error set diffed line by line against a pristine `HEAD` worktree and byte-identical. `check-classroom-curriculum --strict`: no structural findings; the AI Data-Center Wave lane now reads 7 lessons, analyst-visible 6/7, and the drill pool grows to `lc 92 + lq 161 = 253`. `--selftest` 13/0. Rendered at admin with **zero page errors**

The rule says today is EST, so the twelve sections dated 2026-09-13 (eleven plus this one) stay exempt: **108 raw, 96 non-exempt** — the fifth consecutive push at exactly 96. ## [v05.52r] — 2026-09-13 04:35:07 PM EST

> **Prompt:** "continue with your recommendation"

**The sweep found nothing wrong, and then found something else.** All nine guidance modules were searched for the nationality-framed FCC claim that v05.50r corrected in the bankability module. **No other module repeats it** — that is the answer to the question asked. But the search surfaced a collision that v05.50r itself created, and this commit closes it.

### The negative result, stated plainly

Every module was parsed and every string walked against `FCC | inverter | non-Chinese | Chinese-made | Chinese inverter | equipment authoriz | Covered List | SBOM | PCS/EMS`. Hits outside the bankability module were four, and all four are correct as written:

| Module | Hits | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `eo14420-bulk-power-2026-08` | 8 | EO 14420's **own** §5(b) equipment list and §5(c) definition — a different instrument, correctly stated |
| `power-infra-aidc-2026-08` | 2 | NOGRR 245 vs 282 and the IBR glossary entry — correct |
| `bess-tech-fundamentals-2026-08` | 1 | PCS glossary entry (UL 1741 SB / IEEE 2800) — correct |
| `grid-equipment-shortage-2026-09` | 1 | a proscons card title — not a claim |
| `china-policy-stack-2026-08` | 0 | — |

The discrimination that mattered: **FEOC, PFE, §154 and tariff claims are legitimately about Chinese entities and were left alone.** Only the FCC Covered List is nationality-agnostic, and only the bankability module had ever framed it otherwise.

### The collision v05.50r created

`eo14420-bulk-power-2026-08` teaches **§5(c)**: *"foreign-produced" = not manufactured, produced, **or assembled** in the United States* — and its `defs` section already warns that this is "a materially different test from the tax-side domestic-content rules."

v05.50r added a **third** test wearing the same two words, applied to **the same named equipment class**: the FCC's Covered List defines "foreign-produced power inverters" as those **neither** §45X-eligible **nor** a 48 CFR §25.101(a) domestic end product. The two disagree about one physical unit — an inverter given final assembly in the US from foreign subassemblies is **not** foreign-produced under §5(c), and **is** foreign-produced for the FCC unless it clears §45X or the content bar. EO 14420 §5(b) names grid-connected inverters explicitly, so both tests reach them.

The EO module's quiz turns on exactly that fact pattern — *"An inverter assembled in Texas from foreign-made subassemblies. Under §5(c), is it 'foreign-produced'?"* — and a seller who learned only that answer would carry it into an FCC conversation and be wrong.

### Changed

- **`googleAppsScripts/Classroom/Classroom.gs`** (v01.24g → v01.25g)
  - `eo14420-bulk-power-2026-08` — `defs` gains a fifth paragraph naming the FCC test as the third meaning and showing where the two disagree; the `foreign-produced` flashcard reworded to carry all three regimes; a ledger row added citing DA 26-870 and 48 CFR 25.101(a); the Texas-assembly quiz item's `why` extended. `updated` 2026-09-01 → 2026-09-13 with a `revisions[]` entry. **`reviewBy` stays at 2026-12-24** — that is its own §3(b) rulemaking gate and nothing in this edit moves it
  - `bess-bankability-2026-08` — a reciprocal clause in the FCC paragraph of `bankability`, so the trap is visible from whichever module the reader opens first; the existing 2026-09-13 `revisions[]` note extended rather than a second same-day entry added
  - **Section ids, module ids and all three `counterparty` report anchors unchanged** in both modules; the EO module's flashcard count is unchanged at 7 and no card or quiz item was inserted, so **no `gc:`/`gq:` positional index shifted**

### Worth knowing

- **A quiz item's `why` is not part of its drill hash.** `clDrillGuidanceItems_` hashes `q + '||' + c.join('|') + '||' + a` — `why` is excluded. Confirmed in source before relying on it, which is why the quiz explanation could be improved at **zero schedule cost**. A flashcard's hash covers both `q` and `a`, so the one reworded card does re-enter the drill as new; that is the designed rule
- **This commit is the first live exercise of the v05.51r deploy fix.** Run #562 confirmed the fix shipped dark exactly as predicted — it touched no `.gs`, so no deploy step fired. This one touches `Classroom.gs`, so `Deploy Classroom` should now report `Classroom deploy confirmed (GET): Updated to v01.25g …` with no POST attempt at all

### Verification

- `node --check` on a `.js` copy clean; `check-gas-inner-scripts.js` 9 files / 86 blocks; both edited module literals re-parsed as strict JSON with their section-id lists asserted equal to the expected names
- `check-classroom-content.py` **byte-identical to a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (the 24 pre-existing errors on twelve stale `segment-*` lessons, untouched); `check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict` clean; pipeline `--selftest` 13/0 and `--base origin/main` **9 P2, no P3**, so `gateDigest` correctly left alone; `check-profiler-reports.py` 0/0; `check-readme-tree.py` 0 findings after `--fix` synced the GAS display
- **Playwright at admin, zero page errors across both modules.** The new `defs` paragraph, the reworded flashcard and the new ledger row all paint on `eo14420-bulk-power-2026-08`; the reciprocal clause paints on `bess-bankability-2026-08`. The quiz `why` was verified by **actually answering the question** in the harness — it is absent from the initial DOM by design and appears on answer

## [v05.51r] — 2026-09-13 04:08:09 PM EST

> **Prompt:** "If there is nothing to do on my side, then I would prefer you execute steps 1-5 all together. If that would cause any issues, let me know. Otherwise, go for it."

**The GAS deploy path stops reporting success it has not verified.** Investigated from the GitHub Actions logs rather than from the session note, which turned out to be partly wrong about what happened.

### The correction to the record

`SESSION-CONTEXT.md` carried: *"Run #558's Deploy Profiler reported success and ran ten seconds — and the Apps Script project was still on v01.38g."* The run log says otherwise. Line 477 of run #558's `auto-merge` job reads `Deploy confirmed (GET): Already up to date (v01.39g)`, and v05.48r is the commit that *introduced* v01.39g — so the deployment had already advanced to it within the preceding ten seconds, which only the POST at 07:32:25 can explain. **Profiler did deploy at run #558.** The "still on v01.38g" reading was almost certainly a stale Apps Script editor tab; that editor does not live-reload.

What the logs do establish, across three observations:

| Run | Project | POST leg | GET leg reported |
|---|---|---|---|
| #558 | Profiler v01.38→39g | unconfirmed (8s) | `Already up to date (v01.39g)` — POST had deployed |
| #558 | Classroom v01.22→23g | unconfirmed (10s) | `Updated to v01.23g (deployment 32) \| 32/200` — GET deployed |
| #561 | Classroom v01.23→24g | unconfirmed (13s) | `Already up to date (v01.24g)` — POST had deployed |

The mechanism was delivering. It was delivering by luck of having a fallback, and reporting in a way nobody could read.

### Added

- **`.github/scripts/gas-deploy.sh`** (new) — one shared caller for all eight `Deploy <Project>` steps. The verification logic used to be inline, which is precisely how it drifted: response checking and the GET fallback reached four of eight steps and never reached the other four. Eight copies drift; one caller cannot
- **`.github/workflows/auto-merge-claude.yml`** — a new final step in the `auto-merge` job, **`Fail the run if any GAS deploy was unconfirmed`**, placed **after** `Delete branch` and `Sweep stale claude branches`

### Changed

- **The assertion is now version-specific.** `pullAndDeployFromGitHub()` returns `Updated to <v>` or `Already up to date (<v>)`. The old `case` accepted either string **with any version in it** — which cannot tell a real deploy from a stale read of the `.gs` off the GitHub contents API, since a stale read reports the OLD version as current and passes. The version in the answer must now equal the version in the merged `.gs`
- **GET first, POST as fallback** (the brief said "drop the POST"; reversing the order was taken instead, to keep a second path rather than delete one). The POST leg's response is never readable by the runner — Google's 302 drops it — so it printed `POST deploy unconfirmed` on every run, *including the two where it had actually deployed*, at 8–13s per project. Saves roughly 40s per run across eight projects
- **All four laggards brought up**: `Deploy Testauthgas1`, `Testauthhtml1`, `Globalacl` and `MasterACL` were still `curl … || true` — no check, no fallback, no report. All eight steps are now four lines and identical but for their paths
- **A version-ceiling gauge.** The success string carries `N/200`; Apps Script caps a project at 200 versions and past that `pullAndDeployFromGitHub()` returns `DEPLOY HALTED` while the live app silently stops advancing. Warns at ≥170. Classroom is at 32/200
- **`repository-information/REPO-ARCHITECTURE.md`** — the flowchart node `Deploy GAS via curl POST` and the sequence message `curl POST doPost(action=deploy)` both depicted behaviour this commit changes ([PC-REPO-ARCH] #5). The flowchart gains the version-assertion branch and the record-failure path; the sequence diagram gains the return string, the assertion and the deferred failure. **Both pako URLs regenerated and verified by decompressing them back out of the committed file**
- **`.claude/rules/workflows.md`** — a new section documenting the assertion, the GET/POST ordering and, most importantly, **why the failure is deferred and must not be "fixed" by failing in place**
- **`README.md`** — `.github/scripts/` and `gas-deploy.sh` added to the structure tree

### Why failure is deferred rather than raised in place

`Delete branch` and `Sweep stale claude branches` are both gated on `success()`. A deploy step that exited non-zero would leave the `claude/*` branch alive on the remote, and the next session's Pre-Push push-once check would then block on it. So `gas-deploy.sh` records failures to `$RUNNER_TEMP/gas-deploy-failures` and **exits 0**; the gate step at the end of the job reads that file after cleanup has run and exits 1. Red run, clean remote. The rules file says this explicitly so a future session does not "tidy it up" into a bug.

### Verification

- **The fix ships dark and that is unavoidable**: every deploy step is gated on its own `.gs` having changed, and this commit touches no `.gs`, so no deploy step fires on its own run. Compensated by replaying the **real recorded response strings** from runs #558 and #561 against the new matching logic: **10 of 10** — all three real responses confirm; a stale read (`want v01.40g`, `got v01.39g`) is correctly rejected where the old pattern accepted it; and `DEPLOY HALTED`, both `DEPLOY FAILED` returns, an empty response and a Google sign-in HTML page are all rejected
- `VERSION` extraction exercised against **all eight real `.gs` files** — every one yields a version, so no project can hit the empty-`WANT` path that would otherwise fail every deploy. An unreadable `VERSION` is a recorded failure, never a silent pass
- Ceiling gauge exercised at 32, 171 and 200 out of 200, and against a response carrying no `N/200` at all
- `bash -n` on the new script; the workflow parsed with `yaml.safe_load` after every edit; step ordering asserted programmatically — the gate is the **last** step of `auto-merge` and absent from `check-template` (it landed in the wrong job on the first attempt and was moved)
- Both regenerated mermaid URLs decompress from the committed file, first lines `graph TB` and `sequenceDiagram`, each carrying the new content
- `check-readme-tree.py` 0 findings

### What the primary sources said

It **drops the unit-level test for non-residential BESS**, keeping it for residential systems and for non-residential systems with an active **TRPPS**; runs the **installation-level large-scale fire test on every system regardless of unit-level result, with pass/fail criteria**; and adds **intentional ignition of vented gases**. The module's "cell + module + unit is the complete stack" advice — repeated in `what`, `stack`, `ninefortya`, `checklist`, `sales`, a flashcard and a quiz answer — was therefore **false for the incoming edition** and is now edition-qualified throughout
- **The LSFT procedure moved and NFPA 855-2026 names neither route.** CSA Group's own TIA submission (Log 1852) states **ANSI/CSA C800:25** is published and ANSI-approved with the large-scale procedure at **§9.7**, and that because no consensus standard existed when 855's second draft closed, **the method is not defined in the issued edition**; **Annex G.11** says only what the test must show. Errata 855-26-1 (2025-10-13) confirms the edition is issued. No issued TIA on the 2026 edition was found, so the module states the C800 reference as **proposed**, not adopted
- **The FCC inverter rule is not a China rule — the module said it was.** The Covered List entry, added **2026-07-28 (DA 26-786)** and narrowed **2026-08-20 (DA 26-870)** in WC 18-89 / ET 21-232 / EA 21-233, is **"Foreign-produced power inverters, except power inverters which have been granted a Conditional Approval by DoW or DHS"** — expressly *regardless of the nationality of the producer*. Covered = a **UL 1741 utility-interactive** inverter (§§2.1.23, 2.1.52) that contains or can accept remote connectivity over Ethernet, Wi-Fi, cellular, Bluetooth or similar, wired or wireless; non-utility-interactive units are out of scope. **"Foreign-produced" excludes §45X-eligible units and 48 CFR §25.101(a) domestic end products** (>65% domestic component cost through CY2028, >75% from CY2029). Prospective — existing authorizations stand — and OET **DA 26-789** waived permissive-change limits for harm-mitigating firmware. **This kills the module's "pair Chinese DC blocks with non-Chinese PCS/EMS" play outright.** Second-order finding now carried in the module: DoW reasoned the §45X carve-out from §45X(c)(1)(C)/§7701(a)(52), so **the FEOC file and the FCC file have converged onto one document set**
- **AB 303 did not die in April 2025.** The California bill record shows the hearing **postponed 2025-04-02** and the bill **dying 2026-01-31 under Art. IV §10(c)**, filed with the Chief Clerk 2026-02-02 — a live 3,200-ft / 200 MWh siting threat for nine months longer than the module implied
- **BNEF tightened Tier 1 on 2026-08-15**: at least one qualifying project must now be **financed non-recourse by two or more different commercial banks**, and the threshold is 10 MW **or** 10 MWh (the module's slash read as a conjunction). The ~85% Chinese share is left labelled as the **Q2 2026** list and explicitly **not** re-verified — that list is not public
- **Held, now citable to the instrument.** PRC-029-1: **Order No. 909**, Docket **RM25-3-000**, **90 FR 35599**, published 2025-07-29, rule effective 2025-08-28; the standard's **2026-10-01** date is carried in NERC's own bulletin for **Aug 31 – Sep 7, 2026**. Added: **PRC-029-2** under Project 2025-05 answering Order 909's twelve-month directive, which would retire PRC-029-1, plus the phased dates (non-BES IBRs not before 2027-01-01; hardware-limitation documentation within 12 months for in-service units, 90 days for later ones). ERCOT **NOGRR 272 / PGRR 121** approved by the PUCT **2025-11-06**, effective **2025-12-01**, biting on storage resources with an SGIA executed **on or after 2026-04-01**. FEOC **55%** for storage beginning construction in CY2026 confirmed in **IRS Notice 2026-15** (§7701(a)(52)(B)(ii)). EPRI's database runs through 2025-12-31, so the 2018→2025 window and its caveat stand

- `check-classroom-content.py`: **30 lessons / 6 tracks / 142 gate cases, 24 errors, 0 warnings — byte-identical to a pristine `HEAD` worktree**, so the stale `segment-*` lessons are neither caused nor fixed here. Note for the record: the failing set is **twelve** segments / 24 errors, not the ten the §7.12 brief stated
- `check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict`: **no structural findings**, and section 5 now reads **"0 item(s) due for review"** inside the 30-day horizon (today 2026-09-13, horizon 2026-10-13) — the module has left the horizon exactly as the brief predicted
- `check-classroom-pipeline.py --selftest`: 13 fixtures, 0 failures. `--base origin/main`: **9 findings, all P2** (guidance sits outside the content fence — expected for a developer session) and **no P3**, so the gate surface did not move and **`gateDigest` was correctly left untouched**

Screenshots read for `counterparty` and the rewritten `ninefortya`

### Worth knowing

- **Editing a flashcard or quiz item changes `clDrillHash_`, so `clDrillQueue_` treats it as new** and its SM-2 schedule is dropped. Six of nine cards and two of seven quiz items were reworded, and one of each was appended. This is the designed rule (§7.12: "the text moved under the schedule, so the schedule is not evidence of anything"), not a defect — and the appends went at the **end** of each array deliberately, so no existing `gc:`/`gq:` positional index shifted

### Worth noting

- **The deletion decision, stated deliberately as the brief asked.** The `gd-*` renderer did **not** go, because guidance was never its only caller: `gdRenderDoc` draws the public study guides (since v01.74w), the report view builds on `gdTable`/`gdTimeline`/`gdBars`/`gdProsCons`, and the sign-in log uses `gdTable` and `gdCloseBtn`. Deleting the engine would have been deleting three live surfaces to tidy up after a fourth. What went is what only guidance called. Dead CSS was found by diffing class usage inside `<style>` blocks against the rest of the file — five blocks, all of which were live at `HEAD` and became dead in this commit, so none of them is a pre-existing fence being torn down on the side
- **The merge rule, and why "existing Classroom tick always stands" is right in both directions.** The brief requires that a newer Classroom tick is not overwritten by an older Profiler one. The alternative considered was max-date-wins; it is wrong, because if Profiler's date is the **newer** of the two, Classroom's earlier date is the true first completion and moving it forward would fabricate history and suppress every delta in between. Never overwriting satisfies the brief, is correct in the other direction too, matches the "an existing value stands" rule both `gdProgressWrite_` and `clProgressWrite_` already state, and makes the import **idempotent by construction** — a second run finds every tick present and writes nothing
- **The checker caught what the code change implied.** `check-classroom-content.py` asserts `tickable == sorted(readable lessons)` for every tier. Admitting modules to `clProgressValid_` breaks that assertion by design, so the checker moved with the code in the same commit — and the new assertions are stronger than the old ones, not merely accommodating
- **Rotation did NOT fire, and the raw counter is again not what decides it.** `Sections: 101/100` raw, but **five** sections are dated 2026-09-13 EST — the four that were already exempt plus this one — leaving **96 non-exempt**, below the trigger. The brief predicted rotation would be due, on the reasonable assumption that this push would land on a later day; it did not. The clone was unshallowed anyway (55 → 1,145 commits) as the brief instructed, so SHA enrichment is available the moment it does fire. Oldest whole date group is still **thirteen sections dated 2026-09-04**
- **Two render traps, both worth the next session's time.** `HTML_CONFIG.STORAGE_TYPE` on Classroom is `sessionStorage`, and the page's single-tab enforcement **clears it on load** — so a session seeded from `add_init_script` is wiped before `clAdmitted()` reads it. And `_gasPost` is a top-level `function` declaration, so an init-script assignment to `window._gasPost` is **overwritten** by it. Both stubs must be installed *after* the page's own scripts have run. Recorded in the rules file's step 7
